Annual Securities Report

 From April 1, 2023 to March 31, 2024
       (The 112th Fiscal Year)




        Eisai Co., Ltd.
                                                                                                Translation for reference purpose only




As used in this document, references to the “the Company,” “Eisai,” “we,” “us” and “our” are to Eisai Co., Ltd. and, except as the
context otherwise requires, its consolidated subsidiaries.
This document is English summary version of the annual securities report in Japanese which the Company submitted to Director of the
Kanto Local Finance Bureau. In the event of any inconsistency between the documents in the two versions, the statements in the
Japanese version shall prevail.
This document is not reviewed by the Accounting Auditor.
The annual securities report (Japanese version) can be viewed on the company’s website;
https://www.eisai.co.jp/ir/library/securities/index.html

2. History
      The Company was founded in November 1936 by Toyoji Naito, then Managing Director of Tanabe Motosaburo Store Co.,
    Ltd., who established the "Sakuragaoka Laboratory Co., Ltd" in Mikawashima, Arakawa-ku, Tokyo. Five years later, in
    December 1941, Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. was established in Honjo-machi (currently Honjo City), Saitama Prefecture, with a
    capital of 180,000 yen.


        June 1942       Opened the Honjo Plant (name at that time) in Honjo-machi (cureently Honjo City), Saitama Prefecture
        December        Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. merged with Sakuragaoka Laboratory Co., Ltd. and the surviving company is
               1944     launched as Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. The head office is located in Takehaya-cho, Koishikawa-ku, Tokyo
                        (currently Koishikawa, Bunkyo-ku, Tokyo)
        May 1955        The company name is changed from Nihon Eisai Co., Ltd. to Eisai Co., Ltd., as it is known today.
        September       Listed on the First Section of the Tokyo Stock Exchange
              1961
         July 1965      Participated in the management of Sansho Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (company name later changed to
                        Sannova Co., Ltd.)
      March 1966        Kawashima Plant (currently Kawashima Industrial Park) is opened in Kawashima-cho, Gifu Prefecture
                        (currently Kakamigahara City)
        September       Asian holding company (Eisai Asia Regional Services Pte. Ltd.) is established in Singapore
            1979
        November        Misato Plant opens in Misato-mura, Saitama Prefecture (currently Misato Town)
             1981
     January 1982       The Tsukuba Research Laboratories open in Toyosato-cho (currently Tsukuba City), Ibaraki Prefecture
     October 1983       Eisai Chemical Co., Ltd. (currently Kashima Plant) is established in Hasaki-machi (currently Kamisu
                        City), Ibaraki Prefecture
        November        Eisai Research Institute of Boston, Inc. (name at that time) is established in the U.S.
            1987
        September       Eisai Deutschland GmbH (currently Eisai GmbH) is established in Germany
             1989
      August 1990       Eisai London Research Laboratories, Ltd. (currently Eisai Ltd.) is established in the U.K.
         October        Sanko Junyaku Co., Ltd. (later renamed EIDIA Co., Ltd.) signs a business alliance agreement in the
                        diagnostics products business
        April 1992      U.S. holding company (Eisai Corporation of North America) is established in the U.S.
    February 1995       Eisai Pharmatechnology, Inc. (name at that time) is established in the U.S.
             April      Eisai Inc. is established in the U.S.
          October       Eisai Ltd. is established in the U.K.
     January 1996       Eisai S.A. (Currently Eisai S.A.S.) is established in France
              March     Eisai Suzhou Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (currently Eisai China Inc.) is established in China
               April    Elmed Eisai Co., Ltd. is established
        April 1997      KAN Research Institute, Inc. is established (later merged into the Company)
              April     Eisai Korea Inc. is established in South Korea
        June 2002       Eisai Medical Research Inc. (currently Eisai Inc.) is established in the U.S.
        June 2004       Company adopts the "Company with Committees governance system"
         October        European regional headquarters and holding company (Eisai Europe Ltd.) is established in the U.K.
      March 2007        Eisai Manufacturing Ltd. is established in the U.K.
              March     Eisai Pharmatechnology & Manufacturing Pvt. Ltd.（currently Eisai Pharmaceuticals India Pvt. Ltd.) is
                        established in India
               April    Acquired Morphotek, Inc. in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
             October    Sanko Junyaku Co., Ltd. (later renamed EIDIA Co., Ltd.) becomes a wholly owned subsidiary through an
                        exchange of shares
     January 2008       Acquired MGI Pharma Inc. in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
        December        H3Biomedicine Inc. is established in the U.S. (later merged into Eisai Inc.)
            2010
      March 2014        Misato Plant transferred to Bushu Pharmaceuticals Ltd. (Saitama Prefecture)
       November         China regional headquarters and holding company (Eisai China Holdings Ltd) is established in China


                                                               -4-
                                                                                    Translation for reference purpose only




December      EIDIA Co., Ltd. is sold to Sekisui Chemical Co., Ltd. (Osaka Prefecture)
    2015
April 2016    A portion of the Company's business in the field of gastrointestinal diseases in Japan is split off and a new
              integrated company, EA Pharma Co., Ltd., is established by means of an absorption-type split with
              Ajinomoto Pharmaceuticals Co., Ltd.
     April    Sannova Co., Ltd. is sold to Alfresa Holdings Corporation (Tokyo)
April 2019    Elmed Eisai Co., Ltd. is sold to Nichi-Iko Pharmaceutical Co., Ltd. (Toyama Prefecture)
       July   Eisai Center for Genetics Guided Dementia Discovery (G2D2), a discovery laboratory, is established in the
              U.S and begins full-scale operations
April 2022    Moved from the First Section of the Tokyo Stock Exchange to the Prime Market due to the reclassification
              of the Tokyo Stock Exchange market
April 2024    KAN Research Institute, Inc. is absorbed into the Company by merger

3. Description of Business
      The Group consists of consolidated financial report submitting company, 48 consolidated subsidiaries and 1 associated
    company accounted for using the equity method. The Group’s business is comprised of pharmaceutical business and other
    business. In pharmaceutical business, the Group conducts research and development, manufacturing and distribution activity
    for prescription medicines, OTC products and others. The pharmaceutical business is organized into the following five
    reporting segments in this report: Japan, Americas (North America), China, EMEA (Europe, the Middle East, Africa, Russia
    and Oceania), and Asia and Latin America (primarily South Korea, Taiwan, India, ASEAN, Central and South America). In
    line with the reorganization of Japan business in the fiscal year ended March 31, 2024, OTC and others business (Japan) has
    been integrated into Japan pharmaceutical business.

      The relationship among segmentation, main products and main companies is as follows.

II. Status of the Business
 1. Management Policy, Management Environment and Issues that Need to be Addressed
     Statements in the text regarding the future are based on the judgment of the Group as of the end of the current consolidated
   fiscal year.


   (i) Corporate Concept
        The Group defines its corporate concept as “to give first thought to patients and the people in the daily living domain, and to
     increase the benefits that health care provides to them.” Guided by this concept, all directors, corporate officers and employees
     aspire to meet the various needs of global health care as representatives of a “human health care (hhc) company” that is
     capable of making a meaningful contribution under any health care system. The Group’s mission is to increase the satisfaction
     of patients and the people in the daily living domain, and to empower them to realize their fullest life through an hhc
     ecosystem based on collaboration with other industries and groups. The Group believes that revenues and earnings will be
     generated by fulfilling this mission. The Group places importance on this sequence of placing the mission before the ensuing
     results.
        Translating this hhc concept into action, the Group is committed to deepening the relationships built on trust with its
     principal stakeholders, namely patients and customers, shareholders, and employees, while continuously ensuring compliance
     with applicable laws and ethical standards, thereby enhancing corporate value. The Company codified this corporate concept
     into its Articles of Incorporation and endeavors to share its basic concept with shareholders.
        Based on hhc concept, the Group seeks to increase long-term corporate value by creating social impact through efficient
     realization of social good in the form of relieving anxiety over health and reducing health disparities.


   (ii) Medium- to Long-term Corporate Management Strategy and Issues that Need to be Addressed
         The Group launched “EWAY Future & Beyond” in April 2021 - a new medium-term business plan.


     1) Medium-Term Business Plan “EWAY Future & Beyond”
          The Group launched “EWAY Future & Beyond”, a medium-term business plan, in April 2021. In “EWAY Future &
        Beyond”, the first five years starting in FY 2021 is “EWAY Future”, while FY 2026 onward is “EWAY Beyond”. The most
        important stakeholders to whom the Group contributes were expanded from “patients and their families” to “patients and the
        people in the daily living domain”. In line with our desire to empower patients and the people in the daily living domain to
        “realize their fullest life,” we aim to evolve into an hhceco (hhc concept + ecosystem) company by creating solutions based
        on science and data in the neurology and oncology fields, where we have our greatest strength and unmet medical needs are
        extremely high, through an ecosystem developed in collaboration with other industries.
          In 2023, we have formulated new materiality to effectively realize social good through the hhc ecosystem. In addition to
        our efforts to realize social good in dementia, oncology, and global health areas, we have identified maximization of human
        resource value and financial strategies as important material issues, and set and identified long-term goals, KPIs and risks for
        fiscal 2030. With these materiality as our compass, we will work to effectively realize social good.

     2) Major Progress and Initiatives under Medium-Term Business Plan “EWAY Future & Beyond”
           Under the Deep Human Biology Learning (DHBL) drug discovery and development structure, which implements drug
        discovery research based on human biology evidence by viewing diseases as a Disease Continuum based on
        pathophysiology, we are promoting drug discovery and development activities, from the establishment and validation of
        drug discovery hypotheses to obtaining regulatory approval, focusing on the five drug discovery domains in which the
        Group can most quickly and deeply access the relevant human biology. Specifically, the drug discovery domains consist of
        “microenvironment”, “proteostasis disruption”, “cell lineage and cell differentiation”, “inflammation, hypoxia, oxidative
        stress with cell senescence” and “elimination of neglected tropical diseases and pandemics”. We aim to be a frontrunner in
        the fields of neurodegenerative diseases, such as Alzheimer’s disease (AD), and refractory cancer, while also to making
        ongoing contributions to global health.
          In addition, we aim to provide value by building an ecosystem that supports people at all stages of life, from the daily
        living to the medical domain, in collaboration with partners such as academia, companies and local governments.


        (a) Dementia Area
             Lecanemab (brand name: Leqembi) received traditional approval from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA)
           for the treatment of early AD in July 2023. Approval was granted in Japan in September 2023, in China in January 2024
           and South Korea in May 2024. Applications have been submitted in 13 countries and regions including Europe, Great


                                                                - 13 -
                                                                                       Translation for reference purpose only




  Britain, and Canada. AD is a progressive disease that requires early diagnosis and early treatment. As a pioneer in the
  field of AD, we are working to establish diagnostic and treatment pathways, including cognitive function testing, amyloid
  β (Aβ) testing (PET, CSF), APOE4 testing, administration, and ARIA monitoring, in the United States and Japan, where
  the product has already been launched. In addition, in Japan, joint development of Aβ blood tests and joint research of a
  blood biomarker-based diagnostic workflow for dementia are in progress with several partner companies, with the aim of
  simplifying the diagnostic and treatment pathways.
    Other development projects based on the AD continuum are also in progress. AHEAD 3-45, a Phase III clinical study
  evaluating lecanemab for preclinical (asymptomatic) AD is currently underway. The Tau NexGen Study (Phase II / III),
  conducted by the Dominantly Inherited Alzheimer Network Trials Unit (DIAN-TU) to evaluate the efficacy of anti-
  microtubule binding region (MTBR) tau antibody E2814, is ongoing in Japan, the United States and Europe. Lecanemab
  was selected as the background anti-amyloid agent in the same study. A Phase II clinical study of E2814 targeting
  sporadic AD is being planned. Phase I clinical study for E2511, the selective Tropomyosin receptor kinase A (TrkA)
  synapse binding regenerant which is expected to help restore the function of damaged cholinergic nerves and prevent
  degeneration, is underway in the United States. In Japan, the Eisai-Keio Innovation Lab for Dementia (EKID) is
  conducting drug discovery and research focusing on discovery of novel drug targets related to maintenance and
  enhancement of the brain’s inherent robustness and protective mechanisms.


(b) Dementia Ecosystem
    Through the dementia ecosystem, we aim to provide solutions ranging from maintaining good health, disease
  awareness and prevention before dementia onset in the daily living domain, to solutions for accurate diagnosis and
  confirmation of the effectiveness of treatments (drug and non-drug treatments) as well as measures that will contribute to
  improving quality of life after dementia onset in the medical domain. In September 2023, Theoria technologies, Inc., a
  wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company, was established as a digital business company, and commenced its business
  activities. Theoria technologies, Inc. will be responsible for the development and provision of Sasaeru, an application that
  helps facilitate communication between people with dementia, doctors, and caregivers in the treatment of dementia, and is
  also aiming to develop a cognitive reduction risk prediction AI etc. for early detection of MCI and dementia.
    In Japan, by utilizing “NouKNOW”, a digital tool (non-medical device), the Company is progressing with various
  collaborations with local governments and other industries, such as insurance, finance and automobile, to expand the
  dementia ecosystem. In China, we offer online medical consultations through Yin Fa Tong, a one-stop online health
  platform from daily life to medical treatment, and are working to reduce health care disparities through the use of digital
  technology. In Asia, we are also expanding our ecosystem with other industries and non-profit organizations, and
  promoting initiatives aiming to increase disease awareness, early detection, and early diagnosis of dementia.


(c) Oncology Area
    While efforts to maximize the value of the anticancer agent Lenvima (jointly developed with Merck & Co., Inc.,
  Rahway, NJ, USA) are progressing in the existing indications such as monotherapy for the treatment of thyroid cancer,
  hepatocellular carcinoma and thymic carcinoma (Japan), and combination therapy with pembrolizumab for renal cell
  carcinoma and endometrial carcinoma, clinical studies (LEAP studies) for several additional indications including
  hepatocellular carcinoma in combination with transcatheter arterial chemoembolization, gastric cancer, and esophageal
  carcinoma are underway. In addition, the development of next-generation oncology products focuses on development of
  E7386, a first-in-class middle molecule agent, for refractory cancers that are resistant to Lenvima and Keytruda, as well as
  the creation of unique antibody drug conjugate (ADC) drugs with eribulin, splicing modulators, and protein degradation
  inducing agents as payloads, the modalities that embody Eisai's strengths in chemistry. We are co-developing ADC’s such
  as MORAb-202 and BB-1701 which use eribulin as the payload, with external partners.


(d) Global Health Area
     The Group considers making efforts to resolve the global issue of access to medicines to be its duty as well as a long-
  term investment for the future. The Group is promoting such efforts proactively under public-private partnerships with
  governments, international organizations, private non-profit organizations and others. For the elimination of lymphatic
  filariasis, one of the neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) endemic in developing and emerging countries, we are providing
  lymphatic filariasis treatment diethylcarbamazine (DEC) tablets to the World Health Organization (WHO) for price zero
  (free of charge). These DEC tablets are manufactured at the Group’s Vizag Plant in India. The Group is committed to
  supply DEC tablets until lymphatic filariasis is eliminated in all endemic countries that need DEC tablets. As of the end of
  March 2024, 2.28 billion tablets have been supplied to 30 countries. Furthermore, the Group is carrying out new drug


                                                       - 14 -
                                                                                                        Translation for reference purpose only




    development for NTDs such as mycetoma, as well as tuberculosis and malaria, under the partnership with the Japan-based
    Global Health Innovative Technology (GHIT) Fund, and non-profit organizations and non-governmental organizations
    with extensive experience in new drug development related to NTDs. We are also involved in disease awareness
    activities. In June 2022, the Group signed the Kigali Declaration for eliminating NTDs and expressed our continued
    support for the elimination of NTDs.

  (e) Maximization of Human Resource Value
       Eisai’s Articles of Incorporation defines employees as one of the major stakeholders and specifies that Eisai endeavors
    to "respect human rights and diversity," "provide full opportunities for growth in support of self-fulfillment," and "create
    an employee-friendly environment" in addition to "ensuring stable employment". In line with this, Eisai has formulated an
    “Integrated Human Resource Strategy” and have been implementing human resource policies with the pillars of “well-
    being including employee health”, “diverse workstyle”, “employee growth”, and “organizational and business growth”,
    that ensure both individuals and the organization grow together. Diversity, Equity and Inclusion (DE&I) is a source of
    Eisai’s innovation and an important approach towards realizing our corporate concept. Eisai is promoting the creation of a
    corporate culture in which people with diverse values can play an active role regardless of nationality, gender, age, or
    other factors.
      Eisai is further promoting the maximization of human resource value over the medium- to long -term through the
    Global Engagement Survey, which is utilized to verify and strengthen our human resource strategy. By publishing an
    annual “Human Capital Report” starting from FY 2023, we are disclosing initiatives and KPIs related to human capital
    linked to our human resource strategy, and continuously working to improve them.

(iii) Target Financial Indicators
      In its "EWAY Future & Beyond" medium-term business plan which started in FY2021, the Group has not set any
  medium- to long-term financial targets for revenue and profits, as they are largely dependent on the social environment and
  the approval status of products under development. Instead, annual business plans are created in detail. The forecast for
  FY2024 is as follows.

  10-year average ROE are targeted.

(3) Basic Policy for Capital Strategy
     The Group’s capital strategy policy is to improve shareholder value based on “medium- to long-term Return on Equity
  management”, “sustainable and stable shareholder returns” and “value-creative investment criteria for growth”, while
  maintaining the integrity of its finances.

  (i) Medium- to Long-term ROE Management
      The Group believes that ROE is an important indicator of the sustainable creation of value for shareholders. In terms of
    medium- to long-term ROE management, the Company aims for an ROE that exceeds the cost of capital (creation of a
    positive equity spread*1) by constantly improving profit margins, financial leverage and asset turnover in the medium- to
    long-term.
       *1 Equity spread = ROE – Cost of shareholder capital




  (ii) Sustainable and Stable Shareholder Returns
        In terms of shareholder returns, profits are returned to all shareholders in a sustainable and stable way based on factors
    such as a healthy balance sheet and comprehensive consideration of the consolidated financial results, DOE*2 and free cash
    flow, as well as taking into consideration the signaling effect. Because DOE indicates the ratio of dividends to consolidated
    net assets, the Group has positioned it as an indicator that reflects balance sheet management, and, consequently, capital
    policy. Acquisition of the Company’s own shares may be carried out appropriately after factors such as the market
    environment and capital efficiency are taken into account. The Group uses the ratio of equity attributable to owners of the
    parent and net debt equity ratio*3 as indicators to measure a healthy balance sheet.
       *2 DOE (Dividend on equity attributable to owners of the parent) = Dividends paid / equity attributable to owners of the parent

       *3 Net debt equity ratio (Net DER) = {interest-bearing debt (borrowings) - cash and cash equivalents - time deposits exceeding 3 months, etc. -


       investment securities held by the parent} ÷ Total equity attributable to owners of the parent


  (iii) Value-creative Investment Criteria
        To ensure that strategic investments create shareholder value, the Group invests selectively using its Value-creative
    Investment Criteria based on Net Present Value and the Internal Rate of Return spread using a risk-adjusted hurdle rate.




          Notes:
          (1) FAM: Fixed Asset Monetization
          (2) GCMS: Global Cash Management System

                                                                                               Translation for reference purpose only




2. Approach and Initiatives Related to Sustainability
    The Group’s approach and initiatives related to sustainability are as follows.
    Please note that matters related to the future mentioned in the text are based on the judgment of the Group as of the end of the
  current consolidated fiscal year.


  (1) "Governance" and "Risk Management" related to Overall Sustainability
    1) Governance
        The Company adopts a system with a nomination committee, etc., and has clearly separated the oversight of management
       and the execution of business. The Board of Directors delegates decision-making authority for business execution to
       corporate officers significantly, focusing instead on management oversight. Corporate officers strive for prompt, dynamic
       decision-making and business execution. The Board of Directors, independent from operational divisions, exercises the
       function of oversight from the perspective of stakeholders to ensure fairness and transparency in management. Recognizing
       sustainability initiatives as a critical management issue and one of the risks impacting corporate value, the Board of
       Directors periodic reports on the Group's status of sustainability initiatives contributing to the enhancement of long-term
       corporate value and monitors this through reports on specific issues from the responsible corporate officers. The hhc
       Governance Committee has established a subcommittee to check on the status of sustainability initiatives in order to enhance
       discussions on sustainability at the Board of Directors meetings, thereby facilitating the communication of information and
       discussion with relevant corporate officers. The status of the subcommittee discussions is promptly reported to the hhc
       Governance Committee.
         In operational divisions, various committees are chaired by corporate officers responsible for specific topics, such as the
       Company-wide Environment and Safety Committee and the Human Rights Awareness Committee, has been established to
       address the Company’s sustainability related issues including ESG. When dealing with broad topics, such as human rights
       issues which companies should respect, cross-organizational projects are established with multiple corporate officers
       discussing and deciding on the issues. If higher-level decision-making is required, the issue is referred to the Executive
       Board. The results of these deliberations are reported to the Board of Directors promptly and appropriately in accordance
       with each corporate officer’s obligations.
         In addition, the Company has established the “Sustainability Department” dedicated to sustainability, whose mission is to
       work toward the realization of a sustainable society and the long-term enhancement of corporate value. A system has also
       been put in place to oversee the individual sustainability issues addressed by each department and the various external
       environments surrounding the Company, enabling the entire company to work together to promote initiatives. The
       Sustainability Department is also responsible for developing and implementing initiatives related to improving access to
       medicines in the area of Global Health, which the Company positions as an important contribution to sustainability.
         Further, the Company has also established the “Sustainability Advisory Board” comprised of external experts as an
       advisory council body to the CEO. The Board aims to obtain highly qualified advice on access to medicines and variety of
       sustainability-related issues.




    2) Risk Management
         Regarding the Company’s risk management, please refer to "IV Status of the Company, 4. Status of Corporate
       Governance, etc., (1) Overview of Corporate Governance, (iii) Other Matters Related to Corporate Governance, (A) Status
       of establishment of an internal control system and a risk management system, b) Compliance risk management, ii)
       Promotion of risk management."

                                                                                                         Translation for reference purpose only




(2) "Strategy" and "Indicators and Targets" Related to Sustainability
  1) Initiatives to Improve Access to Medicines
        The Group considers making efforts to resolve the global issue of access to medicines to be its duty, as well as a long-
     term investment for the future. It has been promoting such undertakings proactively under public-private partnerships with
     governments, international organizations, and non-profit organizations, etc.
     (A) Elimination of lymphatic filariasis (LF): LF is one of the neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) that is endemic in
          developing and emerging nations. Eisai manufactures LF medication, diethylcarbamazine (DEC) tablets, at the
          Company’s Vizag Plant in India. Until LF is eliminated in all endemic countries requiring DEC, we will continue to
          provide the tablet to the World Health Organization (WHO) free of charge. By the end of March 2024, Eisai has
          supplied 2.28 billion tablets to 30 countries. Through the WHO "Global programme to eliminate lymphatic filariasis,"
          LF has been eliminated in 19 of 72 endemic countries (of the 19, the Company has provided DEC tablets to 5
          countries).
     (B) Ongoing support to NTDs: Eisai was the only Japanese company to participate in the London Declaration on Neglected
          Tropical Diseases, announced in 2012. The London Declaration is an international public-private sector partnership
          working toward the elimination of 10 NTDs. As such, we have been engaged in efforts to eliminate NTDs, including
          LF. Eisai also signed the Kigali Declaration, announced in 2022, which is the successor to the London Declaration and
          aligns with the WHO's NTDs Roadmap 2021-2030. It aims to eliminate NTDs through enhanced ownership by
          endemic countries and further strengthened partnerships with domestic and international stakeholders. Since the
          London Declaration, activities to eliminate the diseases through global partnerships have led to the elimination of at
          least one NTD in 50 countries, and treatment interventions reaching 850 million people in 2022. However, 1.62 billion
          people worldwide are still at risk of infection by NTDs. Eisai will continue to support the elimination of NTDs through
          the free provision of DEC tablets and the development of new drugs for NTDs.
     (C) Contribution to the fight against the most neglected tropical disease, mycetoma: Since 2019, Eisai has supported and
          cooperated with the Japanese international NGO, Association for Aid and Relief, Japan (AAR Japan), which operates
          in Sudan (currently suspended due to civil war). Furthermore, Eisai conducted clinical trials for mycetoma in Sudan in
          collaboration with the Drugs for Neglected Diseases initiative (DNDi) with the in-house antifungal agent E1224
          (fosravuconazole). Preparations are currently underway for a new drug approval application for E1224 in Sudan.
     (D) Development of new drugs for the treatment of NTDs and malaria: Through partnerships with international research
          agencies, etc., we are proactively undertaking the development of new drugs for the treatment of NTDs and malaria.
          Using grants from Japan's Global Health Innovative Technology Fund (GHIT Fund) and other sources, we continue to
          develop new drugs for NTDs and malaria through partnerships that leverage the ideas of university researchers and
          nonprofit research organizations specializing in tropical diseases, such as DNDi and the Medicines for Malaria Venture
          (MMV).

  (ii) Business Activities with Consideration for the Global Environment
        Under the hhc concept, the Group considers the global environment as the foundation for business activities aimed at
     achieving social good. It is the Company's responsibility to achieve the social good of relieving anxiety over health and
     reducing health disparities, along with environmental conservation. In addition to the Company's environmental activity
     guidelines (the "ENW Environmental Protection Policy"), in FY2022 we established the "Eisai Environmental Management
     Vision". This vision clearly indicates the Company's commitment to contributing to promoting the formation of a carbon-
     neutral society by reducing greenhouse gas emissions, a recycling-oriented society through proper waste management and
     the efficient use of resources including water and a society that coexists with nature through initiatives to protect
     biodiversity, in addition to ensuring proper management of chemical substances. Amid increasing societal demands for
     protection of the global environment, the Company will fulfill its corporate social responsibility as a participant in the
     United Nations Global Compact by pursuing Company-wide efforts to reduce environmental impact in every stage of
     business operations, and by working to achieve the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) adopted by the United Nations
     General Assembly.
       As a results of these efforts, Eisai received an "A-" rating, the second highest of eight levels, in both "CDP(*1) Climate
     Change Report 2023" and "CDP Water Security Report 2023." We were also selected for ranking in the "Carbon Clean
     200"(*2), which lists the top 200 publicly traded companies worldwide contributing to the reduction of fossil fuel energy
     consumption, for four consecutive years. In addition, we were included in the S&P/JPX Carbon Efficient Index. Moreover,
     for three consecutive years, we received the highest rating of "A" in the "JRECO Fluorocarbon Countermeasures Rating" of
     prime market listed companies, as well as other outside praise and recognition.
     *1: An NGO (U.K.) that requests climate change, water, and forest-related information disclosure from companies with high aggregate market value



                                                                    - 18 -
                                                                                                          Translation for reference purpose only




  and municipalities, analyzes and evaluates it, and discloses it to investors, companies, and governments.
  *2: Selected by Corporate Knights (Canada), a media and research company, with the NGO As You Sow (U.S.).
  (A) Initiatives toward a recycling-oriented society
    The Group aims to contribute to the establishment of a recycling-oriented society through the sustainable use of resources,
  including water.
    In regard to sustainable use of water, Eisai set a medium-term target of reducing the use of water by 7% (by revenue
  intensity) by FY2030 compared to the level in FY2023 through promotion of water usage reduction via recycling and other
  efficient ways of using water resources. In addition, the Company continues to have zero legal violations by maintaining
  high-quality wastewater treatment that contributes to water quality conservation and complying with environmental
  standards related to water, in line with the medium-term target of maintaining zero legal violations until FY2030. Further, in
  FY2023, we established Internal Water Pricing (IWP), which incorporates water usage reduction into investment judgment
  criteria as the investment effect amount by converting the volume of water usage reductions into monetary amounts, in each
  country where we have major sites.
    In regard to the recycling of resources, Group companies in Japan are promoting the reduction of waste generation,
  improvements in recycling rates, and reductions in final disposal volumes. We expect to achieve zero emissions, with the
  ratio of final disposal volume to waste generation volume being 0.5% or less for the 17th consecutive year in FY2023.


  (B) Initiatives for Biodiversity Conservation
    The Group aims to contribute to the realization of a society that coexists with nature, based on harmony with the global
  environment, through the sustainable use of biological resources and the conservation of biodiversity. In August 2020, we
  established the "Biodiversity Guidelines" and set a medium-term target to identify and conserve important species at each
  business site by FY2030. In FY2023, we identified the following as medium- to long-term risks that the Company's business
  activities pose to biodiversity.
  i) The impact of emissions of hazardous substances into the water and atmosphere on biodiversity in neighboring areas to
  the places where the Company operates.
  ii) The impact of waste generated from the use of the Company's products on the environment as a whole.
  On the grounds of the Kawashima Plant (Gifu Prefecture), we maintain a Japanese garden, rich with nature, and about 700
  types of medicinal and otherwise beneficial plants, including endangered species, are cultivated and conserved in the
  Medicinal Herbal Garden at the Naito Museum of Pharmaceutical Science and Industry. Since FY2020, Eisai
  Pharmaceuticals India Pvt. Ltd. (India) has undertaken a tree-planting program to promote environmental awareness and
  have planted a total of approximately 11,000 trees to date.


(iii) Efforts Related to Climate Change
         After declaring support for the recommendations made by the Task Force on Climate-Related Financial Disclosures
     (TCFD) in June 2019, the Group conducted a scenario analysis as recommended by the TCFD and disclosed the results in
     FY2020. In FY2022 and FY2023, the Group conducted a reassessment of the potential impact of climate change-related
     risks and opportunities on the Group by conducting another analysis that considered multiple climate scenarios. The
     climate-related risks and opportunities, as well as the Company's efforts to address them, are as follows.

          (B) Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions
               Eisai is engaged in initiatives aimed at achieving the globally-shared target of "Keeping the rise in the average global
               temperature to 1.5°C or less compared to pre-industrial levels" (the 1.5°C target), as well as the achievement of "Net
               Zero* by 2050", which is an extension of the 1.5°C target. Since FY2019, the Group has set an SBT 2.0°C target
               (reducing greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions by 30% by FY2030 compared to FY2016). Until FY2022, the Company


                                                                    - 20 -
                                                                                                        Translation for reference purpose only




    achieved its target for 3 consecutive years. Through this, the Company's GHG emissions were successfully reduced by
    over 60% compared to FY2016. Therefore, the Company set an even stricter SBT 1.5°C target which was approved by the
    Science Based Targets initiative in November 2023. In addition, in December 2023, we received approval from the Japan
    Climate Initiative (JCI) to participate in the JCI Race to Zero Circle which is committed to achieving net zero by 2050.
    Further, the Company joined the RE100 initiative, which aims to switch all electrical energy used for business activities to
    renewable energy, and we aim to achieve this target by FY2030.

    <2050 Greenhouse Gas Emissions Net Zero Targets set in FY2023>
    ● Net zero achievement target year: 2050
    ● Greenhouse gas reduction target by FY2030 (SBT1.5°C target)
    - Reduce GHG emissions (Scope 1+2) by 55% by FY2030 from base year FY2019.
    - Reduce GHG emissions (Scope 3 emissions originating from purchased products and services) by 27.5% by FY2030
    from base year FY2019.


    *: Net Zero (as defined by the Science Based Targets initiative’s Corporate Net-Zero Standard)
    • Reducing scope 1, 2 and 3 emissions to zero or to a residual level that is consistent with reaching net-zero emissions at
    the global or sector level in eligible 1.5°C -aligned pathways.
    • Neutralizing any residual emissions at the net-zero target year and any GHG emissions released into the atmosphere
    thereafter.

  (c) Promoting the adoption of renewable energy
      The Group is expanding the adoption of renewable energy by utilizing non-fossil fuel certificates, etc. Overseas, Eisai
    has commenced full-scale operations of a large 3 MW solar power generation facility at the Vizag site (India). In addition,
    facilities such as the Exton site in the United States and the Suzhou Plant in China generate electricity through solar power
    for captive consumption. The European Knowledge Centre (U.K.) also uses green power. These are some of the
    Company's main overseas business sites where we continue to achieve 100% renewable energy usage. Through these
    initiatives, a renewable energy adoption ratio of 99.8% or more of the Company's total power consumption was achieved
    in FY2023. We will continue to introduce renewable energy in the future, and strive to reduce CO2 emissions.
    *: Electric power generated from sunlight, wind power, hydraulic power, and other renewable energy sources

  (D) Efforts related to sales vehicles (domestic Group)
       Group companies in Japan are steadily switching sales vehicles to hybrid cars in order to reduce CO2 emissions from
    sales activities. As of the end of FY2023, the adoption rate of hybrid vehicles has reached 100%. The Company also
    began introducing electric cars in the second half of FY2019, in an effort to reduce CO2 emissions by transitioning to
    vehicles with high fuel efficiency.


(iv) Basic Approach Regarding Maximization of the Value of Human Assets
    The Company’s Articles of Incorporation define employees as important stakeholders in the achievement of its Corporate
  Concept--the hhc concept--and state that the Company will "ensure stable employment," "respect human rights and
  diversity," "provide full opportunities for growth in support of self-fulfillment," and "create an employee-friendly


                                                                  - 21 -
                                                                                                     Translation for reference purpose only




environment."
  Based on the Articles of Incorporation, the Company upholds a global HR Purpose to "unleash the energy of each
individual employee, foster organizational synergy, and maximize social impact," and everyone in the Company aims to
contribute broadly to global society.
  In addition, as we evolve into an hhceco company that contributes broadly to everyone from patients to the people in the
daily living domain, promoting diversity, equity, and inclusion (DE&I) is one of the Company's most critical themes in
striving for further achievement of social good. Since the Representative Corporate Officer and CEO announced the Eisai
Diversity Declaration in 2012, we have worked toward the development of a culture that enables talents with diverse values
to play active roles, regardless of their nationality, gender, age, or other characteristics. In 2023, the Company's
Representative Corporate Officer and CEO articulated a new DE&I message both internally and externally, and developed
the slogan "We see difference, we see potential," which express Eisai's commitment to the hhc concept and DE&I and the
subsequent statement. From FY2024 onward, we are striving to embed this slogan and statement as a shared core value
among all global employees. We are enhancing global collaboration to translate these into specific actions and outcomes,
focusing on improving organizational management to reflect everyday behaviors of each employee and foster synergy.


(A) Ensuring diversity globally
    The Eisai Group has appointed foreign Directors since 2002, foreign national Corporate Officers since 2006, female
  Directors since 2009, and female Corporate Officers since 2013. As of March 31, 2024, out of 31 Corporate Officers, 4
  are foreign nationals and 5 are women. As a global company, we are promoting the recruitment of local talent for
  management of corporations, and have assigned local talents who are Corporate Officers of the Company to management
  positions in various regions such as the Americas, EMEA, and China. At the Headquarters and across global regions and
  divisions, we continue activities to ensure employee diversity through bottom-up committee formation and promotion of
  projects tailored to the challenges experienced in each workplace. As a result, the proportion of women in management
  positions globally is approximately 35%.
    However, in Japan, compared to other countries, there remain challenges in achieving diversity, including gender
  diversity. Various measures are urgently needed to address this issue. Since the Eisai Diversity Declaration in 2012, we
  have implemented, and continue to implement, reforms such as the abolition of general positions in favor of career-track
  positions, expanding recruitment of international students and mid-career hires, establishing a domestic DE&I promotion
  structure aimed at fostering an early cultural shift, and conducting various training programs, mentorship systems, and
  career counseling functions. Particularly critical is the increase in the proportion of women in management positions,
  where we focus on fostering career perspectives for women and nurturing manager candidates. Emphasizing "early,"
  "regular," and "specific," we create opportunities to envision leadership roles early on and implement career support
  measures for both men and women during the childcare phase. As a result, as of March 31, 2024, the proportion of
  women in management positions in Japan was 12.8% and continues to rise.
    Regarding the recruitment of foreign talent at Eisai, we have been hiring international students since FY2013 and aim
  for continuous talent acquisition. Since then, except for FY2014 and FY2017, we have consistently recruited international
  students. We are advancing the acquisition of external talents by appointing mid-career hires to management positions and
  implementing a referral hiring system. Further, in Japan, we continue to actively recruit individuals with disabilities, in
  line with statutory requirements.
     Leveraging our strengths as a global company striving to achieve the hhc concept, the Group integrates knowledge
  from regions and functions outside Japan to promote DE&I globally in a collaborative manner.

(B) Efforts toward maximization of the value of human assets
     Aligned with the Company's integrated human resource strategy aiming for leaps in employee "health," "workstyle,"
  "growth," and "business/organization," we actively invest in improvement of working environment aimed at maximizing
  the energy of employees and creating organizational synergy, in order to maximize the value of talents.


  a) Pursuit of an environment that enables employees to work in a healthy, safe, and secure manner ("Health")
    In June 2019, Eisai issued the "Eisai Health Declaration," prioritizing employee health as essential to achieving the hhc
  concept. We vigorously promote well-being management activities to maintain and enhance employee health. From April
  2022, as part of the Company's second-phase strategic focus, we have set three key initiatives: enhancing health literacy,
  physical health, and mental health. Since 2023, we have conducted health literacy surveys to gauge literacy levels and
  drive various effective measures, aiming to create environments where individuals can keep living vibrantly and true to
  themselves with both physical and mental health.
  <Specific efforts>
    In collaboration with the Eisai Health Insurance Society to help maintain and improve employee health, we decided to
  expand the age range for milestone comprehensive medical examinations in addition to periodic health checkups, and
  introduce predictive health check simulations based on previous health examination results, which have been
  implemented from FY2024. We have expanded the Company's home help support program to partially subsidize costs
  when employees require home helpers and have expanded eligibility for survivor annuity pensions in the event of
  unforeseen circumstances, from the perspectives of both health and peace of mind.


  b) Pursuit of productivity/efficiency through achieving diverse workstyles ("Workstyle")
     Since the concept of "Eisai Innovation (EI)" was announced in April 1990, which stated that “Eisai seeks to be the best
  vessel for all employees to actualize themselves,” we have been advancing initiatives to create a work environment where
  each employee can thrive and to achieve workstyle which can maximize their performance. The Company’s Articles of
  Incorporation explicitly state our commitment to "creating an employee-friendly environment." Embracing the diverse
  environments and values of each employee, we ensure that each employee can approach their work with high
  productivity, health, and authenticity across various work environments with the idea of Work Life Best.


  <Specific efforts>
    We are advancing the creation of diverse workstyles to pursue Work Life Best, aiming for the best of both work and
  life for employees, as well as productivity and efficiency. We promote various employee workstyles such as “workations”
  and vacation systems for continuous leave, side-job systems aimed at self-fulfillment and career building, expanded
  discretionary work systems to enhance individual autonomy, support for the participation of men in childcare, the
  expansion of related systems to support balancing childcare, infertility treatment, and extension of eligibility for welfare
  benefits to common-law and same-sex marriages.


  c) Provision of opportunities for growth aimed at the self-fulfillment of employees ("Employee growth")
     As an hhc company focused on patients and the people in the daily living domain, we continue to implement efforts to
  understand the thoughts and feelings of patients and the people in the daily living domain. To foster the diversity needed
  to meet the increasingly diverse needs of patients and the people in the daily living domain, we support employees in
  autonomously continuing to grow toward realizing their future and achieving the hhc concept and self-fulfillment, and we
  foster a corporate culture that praises their efforts to take on challenges.


  <Specific efforts>
     The Company provides opportunities for employees to engage in "socialization" with patients and their families
  through various training programs. We aim to achieve an environment that allows the dynamic growth of talents who
  build trust with people inside and outside the Company, take on challenges, and continue to provide value to society.
    The keywords are autonomy and taking on challenges. Many of the training programs are held on an open-participation
  basis. Each participant takes necessary actions, with an eye on their own future and on the current situation. After training,
  they are expected to put what they have learned into action, and seek to take on challenges, large and small. We believe
  that there are lessons to be learned from taking on challenges. We are also increasing opportunities for employees to go
  outside the Company. It is an opportunity to question how much value we can create in a world where what we take for
  granted does not apply and how we can co-create with diverse talents. We believe it will be an experience that will greatly
  contribute to achieving hhceco, that is being promoted by the Company. The involvement of other people is also


                                                        - 23 -
                                                                                          Translation for reference purpose only




    important in promoting autonomy and taking on challenges. Thus, we are encouraging one-on-one meetings between
    employees and their supervisors, to enable accelerated individual career formation and personal growth through work. We
    are engaging in new measures, both hard and soft, including the introduction of the special paid vacation system that can
    be used when carrying out self-development and social contribution activities, and holding online dialogue with both
    internal and external members, as a forum for training participants to share their learning.

    d) Sustainable business growth through creating a corporate culture and organizational structure that praises taking on
    challenges ("Business/Organization")
      The Company aims to achieve social good efficiently by having each employee carry out daily work with motivation, a
    sense of achievement, and a strong resonation with hhc concept. In addition, through data-backed, objective decision-
    making, we strive toward evolving into a company that delivers higher social value.

    <Specific efforts>
    i. Improvement of employee engagement
          Central to the Company's talent strategy is anchoring high engagement of employees to the execution of work,
       leading to high productivity and patient/customer satisfaction. The Company introduced a monthly survey of employee
       engagement in May 2020, and holds discussions and continues to make improvements in the workplace based on the
       scores. In FY2021, we introduced a global engagement survey, and are promoting contribution to customers by
       enhancing job satisfaction throughout all Group companies. In the third survey, which was conducted in February and
       March 2024, remarkably 90% of employees responded, and 85% of the respondents indicated high engagement, which
       exceeds last year’s level as well as the benchmark. We will promote data analysis and continue to consider measures
       that improve employee engagement.


    ii. Optimization of organization/human resource management
         To maximize our talent base for executing the Company's business strategy, we utilize qualitative information and
       conduct people analytics (data collection and analysis related to employees and organizations) to visualize employee
       engagement. Leveraging quantitative insights, we perform human talent portfolio analysis and optimal placement
       analysis, executing effective data-driven human resource strategies to maximize performance.
         Further, for critical global positions, annual succession planning processes ensure diversity in selecting, developing,
       and promoting next-generation leaders across nationalities, genders, and age groups.


(v) Human Rights Initiatives
    The Group recognizes human rights as the most universal and fundamental requirement in business operations. We
  promote efforts to respect human rights aligned with international standards such as the UN Guiding Principles on Business
  and Human Rights, OECD Guidelines for Multinational Enterprises on Responsible Business Conduct, and the Tripartite
  Declaration of Principles concerning Multinational Enterprises and Social Policy (ILO Tripartite Declaration). Legislation
  mandating human rights due diligence, such as the EU Corporate Sustainability Due Diligence Directive (adopted by the
  European Parliament in April 2024) and the EU Directive on Corporate Sustainability Reporting (effective January 2023),
  which makes the disclosure of human rights and other sustainability information, applies even to non-EU companies, driving
  global enforcement of corporate human rights commitments. In March 2019, with approval from the Company's Executive
  Committee, the highest decision-making body of business execution, and endorsement from the Company's Board of
  Directors, the "ENW Human Rights Policy" was formulated. The Company’s organization-wide "Business and Human
  Rights Project" undertakes initiatives based on international human rights standards. This project leads the cycle of human
  rights due diligence through identifying risks of potential adverse impact on the human rights of the Company's major
  stakeholders affected by our operations: patients and clinical trial participants, employees, and business partners
  (stakeholders in the supply chain), implementing measures to avoid or mitigate these risks, monitoring implementation
  status and disclosing results.
  (A) Efforts related to human rights in the supply chain
      As the legislation regarding initiatives in each country progress, the Japanese government also issued the "Guidelines
    on Respecting Human Rights in Responsible Supply Chains" as its framework for addressing human rights issues in
    September 2022. As global business expands, Japanese companies are increasingly called upon to mitigate human rights
    risks within their supply chains and business partners. The Group recognizes "human rights in the supply chain" as a
    priority area and actively engages in sustainable procurement targeting both primary suppliers of direct materials and
    secondary suppliers (where primary suppliers are trading companies or wholesalers) within and outside Japan. Through



                                                            - 24 -
                                                                                          Translation for reference purpose only




    sustainability assessments encompassing human rights and labor standards, we strive to understand our business partners'
    efforts toward respecting human rights, identify human rights issues, and enhance engagement with them. In addition, in
    September 2023, we held a business partner briefing for 167 direct material suppliers, which included a lecture by outside
    experts titled "Regulatory Trends in Human Rights and the Environment and Required Actions by Japanese Companies,"
    aimed at raising awareness about the importance of corporate responsibility to respect human rights.

  (B) Efforts to respect the human rights of employees and clinical trial participants
       In clinical research, the Company adheres to ICH-GCP standards to ensure the human rights and safety of participants
    in clinical trials, obtaining informed consent based on the individual's free will. We conduct clinical trials in compliance
    with regulatory requirements in each country, internal standards, and clinical trial plans. In addition, in order to provide
    comprehensive and equitable treatment options, Eisai positions efforts to enhance diversity in clinical trials as a core
    commitment, ensuring expanded access to clinical trials for all patients regardless of ethnicity, race, sex, age,
    socioeconomic status, gender identity, residence, or physical abilities.
      For efforts related to patients' human rights, please refer to "(i) Initiatives to Improve Access to Medicines." For efforts
    related to employees' human rights, please refer to "(iv) Basic Approach Regarding Maximization of the Value of Human
    Assets."


  (C) Human rights education and training
      To promote business activities based on respect for human rights, we conduct various activities for officers and
    employees of the Company and domestic Group companies, including human rights awareness training, seminars, and
    slogan contests. In FY2023, under the theme "Creating a Workplace Where All Employees Can Thrive (For the
    Achievement of Social Good)," the Company conducted human rights awareness training via video broadcasts, with a
    participation of 4,788 employees (participation rate: 93%), including domestic Group companies. Further, we held an
    online seminar on "Human Rights Violations on the Internet and Social Media," inviting individuals who have been
    subject to defamation and harassment from outside parties. A total of 6,336 people participated, via live lectures and video
    broadcasts (participation rate: 123%; includes individuals participating via multiple methods).


(vi) Sustainable Procurement
    Companies are required to engage in sustainable procurement activities that prioritize human rights, labor and safety, the
  environment, and ethics throughout the entire supply chain. Sustainable procurement prevents the occurrence of human
  rights violations as well as issues related to the environment in the supply chain. It also enables the building of a robust and
  sustainable supply chain. In recognition of the efficiency and effectiveness of promoting such initiatives across the industry,
  the Company has been a member of the Pharmaceutical Supply Chain Initiative (PSCI), a global non-profit organization in
  the pharmaceutical and healthcare sector, since 2021. We adhere to PSCI principles and collaborate with our business
  partners to promote sustainable procurement initiatives.
    As part of our efforts to encourage our business partners, we continue to engage by explaining our sustainability policy,
  obtaining their consent to the "Eisai Global Code of Conduct for Business Partners" (hereinafter, "Code of Conduct")
  formulated in accordance with PSCI principles, conducting business partner sustainability evaluations using the EcoVadis
  SAS (France) platform, and providing feedback on evaluation results. These efforts aim to enhance the effectiveness of our
  activities outlined in the "Eisai Global Procurement Standard," established in September 2022, which specify compliance
  requirements for all procurement activities within the Group.
    In regard to direct materials, we expanded the scope of sustainable procurement to include business partners at our
  overseas plants from FY2022. By FY2023, we initiated efforts with major business partners at 3 plants in China, India, and
  Indonesia, including receiving their consent to the Code of Conduct and sharing EcoVadis sustainability assessment results.
  Moreover, in FY2023, we included business partners related to LEQEMBI manufacturing in the United States in our
  sustainable procurement efforts. Regarding direct material business partners at plants in Japan, we held the 5th business
  partner briefing remotely, with 163 suppliers participating. In addition, we engaged in dialogue based on sustainability
  assessment results with our business partners in Japan and overseas to share priority improvement points. For indirect
  materials, when the transaction amount exceeds a certain threshold, we sequentially requested agreement to the Code of
  Conduct from our business partners when new contracts were concluded or contracts were renewed.

    Corporate Governance, etc., (iii) Other Matters Related to Corporate Governance, (A) Status of establishment of an

  (E) Promotions that follow compliance
      The Eisai Group conducts promotional activities globally in accordance with compliance. In order to ensure the broad
    understanding of society that our corporate activities are conducted according to the highest ethical standards, we disclose
    payments to medical institutions, etc., and patient groups in accordance with laws, regulations and guidelines stipulated by
    the Japan Pharmaceutical Manufacturers Association (JPMA) and each respective country.


       a) Stipulation of the Code of Conduct in the Compliance Handbook
          The Group has established guidelines for promoting actions in compliance in the "Compliance Handbook"
       distributed to all employees. The following is an excerpt.

      • Eisai markets and promotes its pharmaceutical products worldwide. We provide accurate and balanced scientific
        information, and promote our products only for the uses for which they have been approved by the applicable
        regulatory authorities.
      • "Promotion" means any activity undertaken, organized, or sponsored by a pharmaceutical company which is directed at
       Healthcare Providers (HCPs) to promote the prescription, recommendation, supply, administration, or consumption of
       its pharmaceutical products through all methods of communication, including the Internet.
      • When engaging in promotional activities with HCPs, we are expected to be familiar with local laws and regulations for
        such engagements in our home country.
      • Promotion in a manner not consistent with the approved label is prohibited and promotion of drugs prior to approval is
        also prohibited. All promotional materials must be reviewed and approved in accordance with local processes and may
       be used only for the approved purpose.

       b) Establishment of the Eisai Co., Ltd. Code of Practice
          In March 2012, the International Federation of Pharmaceutical Manufacturers and Associations (IFPMA) announced
       the "IFPMA Code of Practice" targeting not only marketing activities but also interactions with healthcare
       professionals, healthcare institutions, patient groups, and the promotion of pharmaceuticals. The Japan Pharmaceutical
       Manufacturers Association (JPMA), of which the Company is a member, formulated and implemented the "JPMA
       Code of Practice" in accordance with the spirit of the "IFPMA Code of Practice." The Company has established the
       "Eisai Co., Ltd. Code of Practice" in accordance with the "JPMA Code of Practice." All officers and employees of the
       Group adhere to this Code, ensuring high ethics and transparency at all times, fulfilling accountability for interactions
       with researchers, healthcare professionals, patient groups, etc., and striving to earn societal trust through their activities.


(viii) Efforts Related to Information Security
      While the use of IT and digital technology is advancing, cyber attacks are becoming more sophisticated and devious year
  by year, increasing the possibility of shutdowns and other disruptions to business activities.
    Considering the personal information, undisclosed information, and other types of important information in its possession,
  the Group could see its credibility and competitive advantages suffer if a data breach were to result in a leak of sensitive
  information. In recent years, the corporate community is also dealing with the growing need to respond appropriately to
  global demands for the protection of personal information. The Group is also fully aware that leaks of unreleased structural
  formulas for projects in the drug discovery phase would have a negative impact on the processes for filing and acquiring
  patents. For the Group, a loss of credibility or competitive advantage could have a major impact on business results.
    To prevent the interruption of important business operations and the leakage of personal and confidential information due

  to cyber attacks and other incidents, we newly appointed a Chief Information Officer and are enhancing our IT functions and
  the accelerating the development of our global structure. Further, in addition to strengthening system infrastructure security,
  we have established regulations and other guidelines related to information management, provided officers and employees
  with education on management of information in daily work and learning opportunities such as training on cyber security,
  and are working to continually enhance governance related to global information security and implement related measures.

(ix) Efforts Toward Investment in Intellectual Property
     The legal protection and effective utilization of the proprietary technologies and products the Group has developed
  through our investments in R&D and business activities are indispensable to the sustainable growth and development of the
  Company and the stable delivery of pharmaceuticals to patients. For this reason, we are strategically pursuing various
  activities related to intellectual property, such as patent acquisition.
   For more details on the Group's intellectual property investment efforts, please refer to "II Status of Business, 1.

  3. Risk Factors
           The following is a list of major risks and uncertainties that management recognizes as having the potential to significantly
      affect the financial position, financial results, and cash flows of the Group, among other matters related to the business and
      accounting conditions described in the Annual Securities Report. However, these do not cover all of the risks and uncertainties
      faced by the Group, and it is possible that they will be affected in the future by other factors that cannot be foreseen, or are not
      deemed to be important, at this point in time.
        The risks and uncertainties surrounding the Group are regularly discussed in the Executive Board meetings and other
      decision-making bodies, and we consider ways to take advantage of them as opportunities or reduce these risks and
      uncertainties. The results of these discussions are reported to and discussed by the Board of Directors, and the risks and
      uncertainties described below reflect discussions not only on the executive side but also at Board meetings.
        In addition, it has been approximately 4 years since the spread of COVID-19, and the impact and risk of the infection on the
      Group's business have been deemed insignificant due to the spread of vaccinations, the launch of therapeutic drugs, and the
      weakening of the virus. Therefore, "(4) Others: COVID-19 Infections," which was included in the annual securities report for
      the previous fiscal year has been deleted.
        These are judgments as of the end of the current consolidated fiscal year, and statements in the text regarding the future are
      not guarantees that they will occur or be achieved.


1) Corporate Concept

 Management          The Company has redefined the key players in our corporate concept of human health care (hhc) as “people in
 based on the       the daily living and medical domains” and expanded the key players we should contribute to from “patients and
 Corporate          their families” to “patients and the people in the daily living domain.” In June 2022, the Articles of Incorporation
 Concept            were partially amended to stipulate that the Company’s corporate concept is to give first thought to patients and
                    the people in the daily living domain, and to increase the benefits that health care provides to them. These aims are
                    shared with stakeholders and considered as our “Purpose.” We also believe that the increased benefit to patients
                    and the people in the daily living domain resulting from achievement of these aims will lead to improved
                    performance of the Group and increased corporate value in the long term. The strategic intent of the medium-term
                    business plan “EWAY Future & Beyond,” which started in April 2021, and the establishment of a business model
                    that promotes collaboration with other industries in the hhceco (hhc concept + ecosystem) Declaration issued in
                    May 2022, are also based on the hhc corporate concept. As a company that seeks to effectively achieve social good
                    in the form of relieving anxiety over health and reducing health disparities, our strong motivation based on our
                    understanding of the true needs of patients becomes the source of the Group’s innovation. In addition, we view the
                    importance of promoting the information management/provision, etc., needed to promote further the research and
                    development of new drugs, produce and sell high-quality products, and achieve safe use of pharmaceuticals, on a
                    foundation of controls, aimed at creating patient value, as “Integrity.” This concept is also the building block of
                    our ESG efforts, such as activities for improving access to medicines including free provision of a lymphatic
                    filariasis treatment, and building of a community that coexists with dementia.
                      Accordingly, insufficient permeation of the corporate concept throughout the Group, stagnation of the
                    implementation of management aimed at implementing the concept, and other factors that hinder the full increase
                    of benefit to patients and the people in the daily living domain may have significant impact not only on the Group’s
                    business performance, but also on the improvement of corporate value, including non-financial value.

2) Business Strategy

 Maximizing the         The Group has determined that maximizing the value of next-generation Alzheimer’s disease (AD) treatments
 value of              including the AD treatment Leqembi (generic name: lecanemab) is one of the most important strategies in the
 Leqembi and           medium-term business plan “EWAY Future & Beyond.” As part of that, we are building diagnostic and treatment
 next-generation       pathways that lead from the start of the patient’s examination through to their diagnosis, treatment and
 AD treatments         monitoring. We are also aiming to simplify these pathways along with advancements in blood biomarkers and
                       the development of maintenance treatment and subcutaneous injection formulations. If these cannot be
                       completed, next-generation AD treatments might not sufficiently reach patients and it may not be possible to
                       earn the revenue anticipated in the future.
                        In the United States, the Group also aims to promote access for a wider range of parties, reduce financial
                       burdens, and contribute to the sustainability of the healthcare system by setting prices with transparent
                       explanations based on the concept of value-based pricing. However, if patients’ access to Leqembi is limited by
                       various factors, we may not be able to earn revenue anticipated in the future.
 Maximizing the          The Group and Merck & Co., Inc., Rahway, NJ, USA are conducting multiple clinical trials for the combined
 value of              treatment of the anticancer agent Lenvima and the anti-PD-1 antibody pembrolizumab for multiple cancer types.
 Lenvima               However, if we are unable to obtain the expected results in those combination therapy clinical trials, or if the
                       positioning of Lemvima changes resulting from unanticipated trial results for competing products or their
                       approval timing, it is possible that additional indications for Lenvima will not be achieved at the originally
                       expected timing, the competitiveness of Lenvima will be weakened, and the sales plan for Lenvima will not be
                       achieved. Sales milestones have been established for the revenue obtained through the partnership model for

                       revenue anticipated in the future.
 Partnership            The Group considers partnerships to be an effective means of improving business efficiency and productivity.
 model                 Partnerships are established with the aim of accelerating new drug development through utilization of the latest
                       science and technology, or for efficient resource usage, maximizing business value, and co-developing new
                       solutions with collaborative partners in each region.
                        If differences of opinion arise with partners or changes in the business environment make it difficult for partners
                       to continue their business or to collaborate in pharmaceutical research and development, production, and sales
                       activities that utilize partnerships to deliver pharmaceuticals as well as new solutions for people in the daily
                       living and medical domains, the aforementioned activities may be delayed or become inefficient. It is also
                       possible that unanticipated partnership expenses will be generated due to the impact of foreign exchange
                       fluctuations or other factors, thereby reducing the planned and anticipated profits, or otherwise hindering
                       maximization of business value. In addition, in the event of differences in interpretation of contracts, it is possible
                       that such differences will develop into litigation or mediation between the Group and partners, ultimately leading
                       to dissolution of the partnership. In such cases, business performance may be significantly affected, including
                       the prevention of the creation of new drugs or achievement of revenue in the future as expected.
 Digital               The Group has incorporated the major theme of implementing a digital transformation in all activities in the
 transformation        medium-term business plan “EWAY Future & Beyond,” with the aim of linking the thoughts and feelings of all
                       stakeholders, accelerating problem solving, and executing solid management efficiently based on data. One of
                       our key challenges will be to cause a paradigm shift in all aspects, from dramatically improving the speed of drug
                       discovery and the probability of success through new technologies to providing people in the daily living and
                       medical domains with drugs and other solutions, and achieve a digital transformation by building collaborative
                       ecosystems (hhceco) that pool our special capabilities with those of other industries. The Company will accelerate
                       the Group-wide digital strategy, with the Chief Ecosystem Officer and Chief Information Officer taking the lead.
                       The changes in the business environment caused by the evolution of IT has made the need for digital
                       transformation clear. Any delays in efforts to achieve digital transformation or factors that hinder its achievement
                       may have significant impact not only on the Group’s business performance, but also on the improvement of
                       corporate value, including non-financial value.
