     (iii) Supervision or auditing by outside directors and internal audits, Audit Committee audits, and accounting audits, as well as
          their interrelationship and connection with the internal control division
          The Company is a company with a nomination committee, etc., and outside directors, who constitute the majority of
       directors, entrusts a large portion of the decision-making authority over business execution to corporate officers to the extent
       permitted by laws and regulations in order to devote its attention to the oversight of management. In addition, outside
       directors, who constitute the majority of the Audit Committee, audit the execution of duties by directors and corporate
       officers through the internal control system and conduct audit activities related to Accounting Auditors and internal audit
       departments.
         Details on the interrelationship between the Board of Directors, the Audit Committee, internal audits, and accounting
       audits, as well as their connection with the internal control department, are described in "(1) Overview of Corporate
       Governance (iii) Other Matters Related to Corporate Governance, (A) Status of establishment of an internal control system
       and a risk management system" and "(3) Audit Status.

(3) [Audit Status]
  (i) Status of Audit Committee Meetings
      The Audit Committee consists of 5 members, 3 of whom are outside directors, making up the majority. In FY2023, the
    Audit Committee held 12 meetings with an attendance rate of 100% for 4 members and 92% for one member (attending 11
    out of 12 meetings).
      The duties of the Audit Committee include formulating an annual audit plan, conducting audits required by the
    Companies Act, such as audits of the execution of duties by directors and corporate officers, monitoring and verifying the
    activities of Accounting Auditors, and overseeing the establishment and operation of internal controls related to financial
    reporting as stipulated by the Financial Instruments and Exchange Act. The audit plan stipulates the setting of multiple key
    audit themes based on an assessment of the risks associated with the business activities of the Group and auditing of the risk
    response status of corporate officers. In addition, through information sharing with the internal audit departments, etc., of the
    Group and the corporate auditors of our subsidiaries, we carry out audit activities as stipulated by the audit plan. We also
    keep a close eye on revisions to regulations such as the Companies Act and stock exchange regulations and reflect these
    changes in our activities.
       The main matters resolved and reported at meetings of the Committee are as follows.
       Resolutions: Division of duties among members of the Audit Committee, the Audit Committee audit plan, regulations
                   relating to the Audit Committee, agreement on compensation for the Accounting Auditor, the reappointment
                   or non-reappointment of the Accounting Auditor, personnel evaluations for the Management Audit
                   Department, audit reports, etc.
       Report items: Reports from the CFO and Accounting Auditor on quarterly/year-end financial statements, the Business
                   Report and corresponding Annexed Detailed Statement, reports from corporate officers on their execution of
                   duties, etc.
      Of members of the Audit Committee, Committee Chair Hideyo Uchiyama has expertise and experience as a certified
    public accountant. At the same time, he has abundant experience as the head of an audit firm and a top executive of a global
    consulting firm. He also has a high level of insight into management as well as excellent supervisory ability. The Chair of
    the Audit Committee, along with 2 inside directors who are familiar with the Company's organization and operations, directs
    the Management Audit Department, which serves as the secretariat of the Audit Committee. They collect reports on matters
    related to the execution of duties by corporate officers and monitor and verify the audits conducted by the Accounting
    Auditor. In addition, they report the results of the Audit Committee to the Board of Directors and responds to questions and
    comments at meetings of the Board of Directors.
      Other members of the Audit Committee also express their own opinions as appropriate on resolutions and reports of the
    Audit Committee to enhance the quality of the Committee's activities.
      On June 14, 2024, Takuji Kanai was appointed Chair of the Audit Committee. Takuji Kanai has experience working in
    audit roles in industries such as telecommunications, automotive, pharmaceuticals, heavy industry, food, retail, and railways
    as a certified public accountant and auditor, and possesses extensive experience managing an auditing firm and a global
    professional firm, and therefore possesses a high level of insight and supervisory abilities regarding management.
       In addition, the Management Audit Department, which is independent from operational divisions, has been established as
    a dedicated department to support the Audit Committee. The Management Audit Department handles the following roles as
    the administrative office of the Audit Committee.
      ･ Preparing agenda items and related materials for the Audit Committee and holding prior discussions with the Chair of
       the Audit Committee
       ･ Providing information to members of the Audit Committee in a prompt manner and explaining agenda items in advance
       ･ Providing the necessary information on matters for deliberation by the Audit Committee to directors who are not
       members of the Audit Committee




                                                           - 108 -
                                                                                                   Translation for reference purpose only




      [Independence of the Management Audit Department from the Operational Divisions*]

      ･ The Management Audit Department shall be organized independent of the vice presidents of the Company.
      ･ The director and staff of the Management Audit Department shall perform their duties under the direction of the
      Company’s Audit Committee and members of the Audit Committee.
      ･ The director and staff of the Management Audit Department shall be appointed, reassigned and disciplined by the
        Representative Corporate Officer and CEO of the Company with the consent of the Audit Committee of the
        Company.
      ･ The decision on personnel evaluation of the director and staff of the Management Audit Department will be conducted
      by the Audit Committee of the Company.
       * Excerpt from the "Rules Concerning Items Necessary for the Execution of Duties by the Audit Committee"

  (A) Monitoring and verification activities of the Audit Committee in relation to the Accounting Auditor
      The Audit Committee conducted the following monitoring and verification activities of the Accounting Auditor.
       ･ The Audit Committee received the yearly accounting audit plans of the Accounting Auditor, confirmed the contents,
         and deliberated on whether to approve audit compensation, etc.
       ･ The Audit Committee received explanations of the results of audits, etc., conducted by the Accounting Auditor
         regarding quarterly and year-end financial statements, and confirmed the contents. In addition, the Audit Committee
          obtained information concerning internal control audits.
       ･ As necessary, the Audit Committee attended the individual audits conducted by the Accounting Auditor and
       confirmed the status of implementation of the audit.
       ･ The Audit Committee received reports on matters related to the execution of duties of accounting auditors as
          stipulated in Article 131 of the Rules of Company Accounting, and confirmed the contents.
       ･ In accordance with the stipulations of Auditing Standards Statement 260, a document issued by the Japanese Institute
          of Certified Public Accountants, the Audit Committee obtained regular reports from the Accounting Auditor and
          exchanged opinions regarding important audit procedures and other matters. The Audit Committee also discussed
          "Key Audit Matters" (KAM) listed as required by the Financial Instruments and Exchange Act of Japan, and
          requested explanations of the contents as necessary.
       ･ The Audit Committee evaluated the auditing firm to which the Accounting Auditor belongs, as well as the quality of
         the audits conducted by the Company's engagement partners and the audit team in charge of the Company, in light of
          the various activities of the Accounting Auditor, the results of investigations by regulatory authorities, and other
          information.


  (B) Auditing activities of the Audit Committee in relation to Internal Audit Departments, etc.
      The Audit Committee conducted the following audit activities concerning the corporate officers in charge of internal
    audits and the internal audit department (Corporate Internal Audit Department, etc.), as well as the corporate officers in
    charge of internal control and the risk management/internal control promotion division (Corporate Compliance and Risk
    Management Department).
       ･ The Audit Committee obtained annual audit plans and reports on the results of individual audits carried out by the
         internal audit departments of the Group, confirmed their suitability, and shared information related to Audit
          Committee activities through monthly meetings with the corporate officer responsible for internal audits and the
          Corporate Internal Audit Department. Individual audits include evaluation of internal control over financial reporting
          under the Financial Instruments and Exchange Act of Japan.
       ･ The Audit Committee obtained information on risk-management activities and efforts to promote internal controls
         through regular meetings with the corporate officer responsible for internal control and the Corporate Compliance
         and Risk Management Department. In addition, the Audit Committee received monthly reports on the state of
          operation of the Compliance Counter.

(ii) Status of Internal Audits
      The Company works together with the internal audit departments set up in each region (the United States, Europe, China,
  etc.) by the Corporate Internal Audit Department, which consists of an executive internal auditor and 16 others, and which is
  overseen by the corporate officer responsible for internal audits, to perform internal audits globally.
    In FY2023, we focused on internal audits that contribute to 4 key issues: (i) enabling risk management to support the new
  product Leqembi and business expansion, (ii) strengthening risk management in the supply chain, (iii) developing and
  enhancing the risk management system in response to environmental changes and organizational restructuring, and (iv)



                                                               - 109 -
                                                                                           Translation for reference purpose only




  strengthening the compliance management system to promote sound business activities. In conducting audits, we increased
  on-site audit opportunities while continuing to use remote audits and local outside experts overseas, and carried out internal
  audit activities generally as planned. The results of all of these activities were reported to the Audit Committee, the Board of
  Directors, and the Executive Board.
    In addition, in regular information-sharing sessions with the Accounting Auditor, we collaborated by exchanging opinions
  on the expected role in internal audits and various system revisions coming into effect in FY2024. We also regularly held
  meetings of the External Assessment Committee, composed of outside experts, to receive the latest information on internal
  audits and engaged in discussions aimed at further improving the quality of internal audits. Further, in quality assurance
  review conducted by the Committee in FY2023 we received an assessment that our internal audit activities were of high
  quality and conformed to the global standards of The Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA, headquartered in the United States).


(iii) Status of Accounting Audits
  (A) Name of audit firm
       Deloitte Touche Tohmatsu LLC
  (B) Continuous audit period
       33 years
  (C) Certified public accountant conducting the work
       The following 3 certified public accountants conducted the accounting audit work for Deloitte Touche Tohmatsu LLC.
        In regard to the Accounting Auditor, the Articles of Incorporation do not stipulate the conclusion of a limitation of
     liability contract, so such a contract has not been concluded.

                                                                                                      No. of years as auditor for
                   Name                                         Position
                                                                                                            the Company
              Yasuteru Miura           Designated limited liability partner, engagement partner                 4 years
             Teppei Yamamoto           Designated limited liability partner, engagement partner                 3 years
              Mikihiko Okabe           Designated limited liability partner, engagement partner                  1 year

  (D) Configuration of individuals assisting with audit work
       The individuals assisting with audit work consist of 12 certified public accountants and 45 others.
  (E) Method and reasons for selecting the audit firm
       The Audit Committee considers the "Policy on Decisions to Dismiss or not to Re-elect Accounting Auditor" to be a
     regulation governing Audit Committee operations and reviews the Policy on a yearly basis. The following resolutions
     were approved at the April 2023 meeting of the Audit Committee.
      In order to ensure the appropriateness and reliability of accounting audits, the Audit Committee of the Company
     monitors the Accounting Auditor to verify that its independence is maintained and that it is performing its auditing duties
     properly. The monitoring and verification involve examining the content of the Accounting Auditor’s audit plan, the audit
     fees and other considerations paid to the Accounting Auditor, the suitability of the individuals conducting the audit, the
     appropriateness of the contents of the audit agreement, notifications from the Accounting Auditor regarding the "structure
     for ensuring that the Accounting Auditor’s duties are being carried out properly" (provisions set forth in each item of
     Article 131 of the Rules of Company Accounting), and past audit performance, among other factors. The Accounting
     Auditor is additionally required to report, in a timely fashion, any obstacle to the performance of its duties, including
     orders received from regulatory authorities to suspend audit work.
       As a result of the Audit Committee’s monitoring and verification, in the event that the Accounting Auditor is
     reasonably expected to fall under Article 337, Paragraph 3, Item 1 or is deemed to fall under the provisions set forth in the
     Items in Article 340, Paragraph 1 of the Companies Act, the Accounting Auditor will be dismissed upon unanimous
     agreement of all members of the Audit Committee. In such cases, a member of the Audit Committee assigned by the
     Committee will report the dismissal and the reason therefor at the first General Meeting of Shareholders convened
     following the dismissal.
        Through the aforementioned monitoring and verification, the Audit Committee evaluates each year the quality of the
     Accounting Auditor’s audits and the effectiveness and efficiency with which it performs its auditing duties, and considers
     whether to re-elect or withhold the re-election of the Accounting Auditor. In the event that a motion to withhold re-
     election of the Accounting Auditor is to be put forth in a proposal at the General Meeting of Shareholders, a member of
     the Audit Committee assigned by the Committee will present all necessary explanations concerning the proposal at the
     General Meeting of Shareholders.
       If a new Accounting Auditor needs to be elected following the decision to dismiss or withhold re-election of the


                                                          - 110 -
                                                                                        Translation for reference purpose only




    Accounting Auditor, the Audit Committee will first confirm that the applicable audit firm do not fall under each item of
    Article 337, Paragraph 3 and of Article 340, Paragraph 1 of the Companies Act. Then it will evaluate a number of audit
    firm with regard to the status on provisions set forth in each item of Article 131 of the Rules of Company Accounting,
    past audit performance and audit fees with global corporations, and other matters, and select a candidate to be proposed at
    the General Meeting of Shareholders.
      In its May 2024 meeting, the Audit Committee concluded that the results of evaluation of the Accounting Auditor for
    the fiscal year ended March 2024 in accordance with the aforementioned "monitoring and verification activities of the
    Audit Committee in relation to the Accounting Auditor" indicated that there were no reasons to dismiss the Accounting
    Auditor for the fiscal year ending March 2025.
  (F) Evaluations of Accounting Auditor by the Audit Committee
      The Audit Committee evaluates audit firm and certified public accountants in charge of audits from different
    perspectives. In evaluating audit firm, the Committee focuses on examining the various internal controls that are put in
    place and operated by the target accountants from the perspective of evaluating the organization, and obtaining the results
    of audit firm evaluations by government bodies.
      On the other hand, in the evaluation of certified public accountants, the independence and expertise of the engagement
    partners in charge are confirmed by the Audit Committee through "monitoring and verification activities of the Audit
    Committee in relation to the Accounting Auditor."

  (D) Policy for determining auditor remuneration
       Three Audit Committee members (selected by the Audit Committee) finalized the Accounting Auditor's audit plan
    (including the labor requirements for the audits) after receiving explanations from the Accounting Auditor and confirming
    the content accordingly. With Audit Committee members present, operational divisions negotiated with the Accounting
    Auditor on the corresponding unit labor costs and calculated a proposed audit fee based on the audit plan.

(E) The Audit Committee’s rationale for agreeing to the amount of compensation, etc., for Accounting Auditor
     In addition to assessing the reasonableness of the calculation of the proposed auditor remuneration and the content
  thereof, the Audit Committee also evaluated past trends in audit fee amounts and the audit fees at other companies from a
  comprehensive perspective to determine whether the compensation and other conditions for the Accounting Auditor are
  appropriate. After completing its assessments, the Audit Committee approved the amount of compensation and other
  conditions for the Accounting Auditor.

(4) [Remuneration for Officers]
 (i) Details of the Policy Concerning the Determination of the Amount of Compensation Paid to Corporate Executives or its
 Calculation Method, and the Method of Determination
      Compensation paid to directors and corporate officers is determined by the Compensation Committee. All members of the
    Company's Compensation Committee are outside directors, and the Committee places emphasis on an objective perspective
    and transparency.
       The Compensation Committee has the authority to determine the details of the individual compensation of the Company's
    directors and corporate officers, primarily with respect to the following matters.


      (i) Policies related to determining the content of compensation, etc., for individual directors and corporate officers
      (ii) The content of compensation, etc., for individual directors and corporate officers
      (iii) Assessing the attainment of corporate officers' Company-wide and individual performance targets, based on
      which their performance-based compensation is determined

    (A) Compensation, etc., of directors and corporate officers
        The Compensation Committee examines various issues concerning the compensation paid to directors and corporate
      officers, confirms the level of compensation each year, and determines the compensation system for the following year.
      The Compensation Committee actively incorporates and utilizes data and other information from external professional
      organizations (such as the "Executive Compensation Database" of Willis Towers Watson) in examining various issues
      related to compensation, etc., and in researching and examining the level of compensation, etc.

      a) Compensation system for directors
         i) Basic policy concerning compensation, etc., paid to directors
           The basic policy concerning compensation, etc., paid to directors shall be as follows.
             Set the compensation, etc., of directors so that the contents are suitable to motivate them to fully carry out their
             management oversight function, which is their duty, in order to improve the common interests of stakeholders and
             increase long-term corporate value. A portion of compensation, etc., paid to directors consists of stock, from the
             perspective of sharing the same profit-consciousness as our shareholders.


         ii) Compensation system for directors
           ･ Compensation, etc., paid to directors is only a fixed base compensation (cash and stock).




           ･ The level of base compensation of outside directors and inside directors is aimed at the upper middle range for the
           industry.
           ･ The chair of the Board of Directors and each Committee chair receive additional compensation for his or her
           service as chair.
           ･ The proportion of compensation, etc., for directors is as follows.




                                                           - 113 -
                                                                                                 Translation for reference purpose only




b) Compensation system for corporate officers
  i) Basic policy concerning compensation, etc., paid to corporate officers
     The basic policy concerning compensation, etc., paid to corporate officers shall be as follows.
       1. Set the contents of the compensation, etc., of corporate officers to be competitive, with the importance and
           heaviness of the duties assigned to the corporate officer sufficiently reflected. This will make it possible to
           contribute to achievement of the hhc concept, attract excellent human resources that can play an active role
           globally, and improve the morale of corporate officers regarding execution of business.
       2. Determine the compensation, etc., of corporate officers with weight placed on performance/outcomes obtained
           as a result of fulfilling the Company's Charter of Business Conduct as an hhceco company as stipulated in the
           Articles of Incorporation. This will increase the convincingness of the compensation of members of the
           management team.
       3. Set the contents of the compensation, etc., of corporate officers so that they are strongly motivated to
           contribute not only to short-term performance based on the results of each fiscal year, but also to
           improvement of the Company's medium- to long-term corporate value, achievement of social good, and the
           sustainability of society. This will respond broadly to the expectations of stakeholders and contribute to
           achievement of the Corporate Concept.
       4. Determine compensation, etc., of corporate officers using objective and suitable evaluation criteria and a fair
           and transparent process, after setting appropriate performance targets and incentives that strike a balance
           between "risk, return, and impact."* Through this, set fair and convincing contents of compensation and
           motivate corporate officers to take on challenges, while being accountable to stakeholders.
       * Risks (e.g., aggressive investment of resources in R&D, etc.), returns (Company-wide financial performance

           indicators), impact (social impact of business activities)

  ii) Compensation system for corporate officers
      The compensation, etc., of corporate officers consists of base compensation (fixed amount) and performance-
    based compensation (variable amount) in the form of bonuses and stock-based compensation (portion granted during
    term of office and portion to be granted upon retirement).




       The proportion of compensation, etc., for corporate officers is as follows.




    * The ratio of the base compensation and performance-based compensation of corporate officers from overseas subsidiaries is set in

    accordance with the market data for each country, so actual amounts may differ from those given in the figure.


       Compensation, etc., for corporate officers shall be determined according to global job grade, aimed at a level in the
    mid- to upper range within the industry so that it will be sufficiently competitive to reflect the importance and weight
    of responsibility of the duties assumed by the corporate officers.




                                                           - 114 -
                                                                                Translation for reference purpose only




  Job Grade of Corporate Officers
  ･ Corporate officers’ job grades indicate the level of responsibility demanded of each job, and serve as the
     standards for determining compensation.
  ･ Corporate officers’ job grades shall be determined by measuring the job grades according to objective methods
     using Willis Towers Watson's global grading system, and then incorporating the Company's own evaluation
     of job responsibilities, etc., in the job grade.
  In order to sufficiently reflect Company-wide performance as compensation of managements, the ratio of
performance-based compensation to total compensation is aimed to be 50% or more, with a higher ratio the higher
the job grade.

1) Bonuses
    Bonuses consist of bonuses that are determined based on the degree of attainment of Company-wide
  performance targets (Bonus A) and bonuses that are determined based on the degree of attainment of individual
  performance targets (Bonus B). The ratio of the base amount for calculation of Bonus A and Bonus B shall be
  50:50.




    The degree of attainment of Company-wide performance targets for Bonus A is determined based on an
  evaluation of financial indicators (return) and non-financial indicators (risk and impact), and Bonus A is paid in a
  range of 0% to 250%.




    The degree of attainment of individual performance targets for Bonus B is determined based on an evaluation of
  the individual performance targets, and the Bonus B is paid in the range of 0% to 150%. The individual
  performance targets of all corporate officers include at least 20% of the targets in the following aspects as social
  good targets for the realization of the corporate image stipulated in the Articles of Incorporation.



                                                 - 115 -
                                                                                                     Translation for reference purpose only




                          1. DE&I (Diversity, Equity, & Inclusion) initiatives
                          2. Ensuring cyber security to protect patient information and ensure a stable supply
                          3. Contributing to social impact through improved access to pharmaceuticals


     2) Stock-based compensation
          Corporate officers’ stock-based compensation is linked to medium- to long-term (3-year) performance, and
        consists of a portion to be granted at the end of the period subject to evaluation (portion granted during the term of
        office) and a portion that vests at the end of each 1-year term of office and is granted upon retirement (portion to
        be granted upon retirement).




          The portion granted during the term of office is performance-based compensation that can reflect medium- to
        long-term performance and contributions to ESG. The 3 evaluation indicators are listed in the table below, and are
        designed to ensure objectivity and transparency by balancing the concept of performance targets of "risk, return,
        and impact" as stated in the Basic Policy on Compensation, etc. for Corporate Officers, and by reflecting medium-
        to long-term performance in a simple and appropriate manner. The evaluation period is set at 3 years. The portion
        granted during the term of office ranges from 0% to 150% based on the attainment of these goals.




     * Each year, the number of shares that will form the basis for the granting of shares (the basic number of shares for calculation) is
        determined, and the shares are granted after the completion of the 3-year evaluation period, reflecting the evaluation of performance.


           The portion granted upon retirement is vested each year that the corporate officer has served a full term of office
        after taking office, accumulated and managed annually for the duration of the corporate officer's term of office,
        and granted upon retirement as an officer. If the term of office as an officer is less than 3 years, the officer is not
        eligible.


c) Malus and clawback clauses
    In the event that a director or corporate officer violates relevant laws, regulations, or internal rules and on certain
  other grounds, the Compensation Committee may, based on a resolution of the Compensation Committee, reduce its
  base compensation and performance-based compensation, suspend payment of such compensation, or demand a refund.


                                                              - 116 -
                                                                                                 Translation for reference purpose only




(B) Determination of performance-based compensation for FY2023 (from April 1, 2023, to March 31, 2024)
     Regarding the performance-based compensation for corporate officers for FY2023, the Compensation Committee
  deliberated on the performance evaluation of the corporate officers and the individual amounts of performance-based
  compensation (bonuses and stock-based compensation) to be paid or granted. The combined total of bonuses was
  determined based on the degree of achievement of Company-wide performance targets (Bonus A) and individual
  performance targets (Bonus B). Stock-based compensation was determined based on the degree of achievement of
  performance indicators for the portion granted during the term of office and the portion to be granted upon retirement.


  a) Bonuses
     i) Evaluation by Company-wide performance target
         Bonus A is determined according to the degree of achievement of both financial and non-financial Company-wide
       performance targets by corporate officers.
         The degree of achievement of Company-wide performance targets (financial) is determined in accordance with an
       evaluation of consolidated revenue, consolidated operating profit, consolidated profit for the year (attributable to the
       parent company), and consolidated ROE. The Compensation Committee evaluated the achievement of Company-
       wide performance targets (financial) in a range of 0% to 250%, in accordance with the degree of achievement of each
       item in FY2023. The Company decided to adopt these 4 evaluation indicators because they represent management
       indicators to be shared with shareholders as published numerical targets for business plan achievement for the
       corresponding fiscal year, and because the Company considers consolidated ROE an important indicator of sustained
       creation of value for shareholders.
         The targets and actual results for the Company-wide performance targets (financial) for FY2023 are as shown in
       the table below. The Compensation Committee deliberated at the Committee meeting held on May 29, 2024, and the
       degree of achievement was determined to be 117% based on the evaluation criteria set at the time the targets were
       set.

  ii) Evaluation of individual performance targets
       Bonus B, which is calculated based on the degree of achievement of the individual performance targets of each
     corporate officer, is proposed by the Representative Corporate Officer and CEO and reviewed by the Compensation
     Committee. The degree of achievement is evaluated within a range of 0% to 150%. Each corporate officer sets
     concrete performance targets, weighted points are assigned to the targets in accordance with their priority, and after
     deliberation with the Representative Corporate Officer and CEO, the individual performance targets are proposed to
     the Compensation Committee. The targets are then determined by the Compensation Committee after evaluation of
     their suitability.
       At its meeting held on May 29, 2024, the Compensation Committee deliberated on the degree of achievement of
     the individual performance targets of each corporate officer for FY2023, and the average degree of achievement for
     all corporate officers was 103.3%.
        Bonuses shall be the sum of the bonus that ais determined based on the degree of achievement of Company-wide
     performance targets (Bonus A) and the bonus that is determined based on the degree of achievement of individual
     performance targets (Bonus B). The ratio of the base amount for calculation of Bonus A and Bonus B shall be 5:5.
       At its meeting held on May 29, 2024, the Compensation Committee determined the bonus based on the
     achievement of Company-wide performance targets (Bonus A) and the bonuses based on the achievement of
     individual performance targets (Bonus B), and the bonuses for each corporate officer for FY2023 were decided.


b) Stock-based compensation
   i) Stock-based compensation (for the portion granted during the term of office)
       In the case of stock-based compensation (portion granted during the term of office), the degree of achievement of
     medium- to long-term targets was evaluated by the Compensation Committee within a range of 0% to 150% based
     on ESG EBIT, relative PBR, and the achievement of each of the Company-wide materiality items for FY2023. The
     reason that these 3 evaluation indicators were adopted for the determination of stock-based compensation is that the
     Company considers them to be indicators that ensure objectivity and transparency by balancing the concept of
     performance targets of "risk, return, and impact" as stated in the Basic Policy on Compensation, etc. for Corporate
     Officers, and by reflecting medium- to long-term performance in a simple and appropriate manner. ESG EBIT
     reflects R&D expenses (investment risk), personnel expenses (investment risk), and operating profit (return). The
     relative PBR serves as an indicator of improving shareholder value. Company-wide materiality comprises 5 items:
     realizing social good in the areas of dementia, oncology, and global health; maximizing the value of human assets;
     and financial strategy, which encompass all aspects of risk, return, and impact.

       The evaluation indicators and methods for the medium- to long-term targets for FY2023 are as follows. The
     Compensation Committee deliberated on the evaluation criteria at the time the targets were set, at the meeting held
     on May 29, 2024. The degree of achievement was determined to be 20%, and the stock-based compensation of each
     corporate officer for FY2023 (portion granted during the term of office) was subsequently set.

      Stock-based compensation (portion granted during the term of office) will be granted 3 years later, at the end of
  FY2025 (July 2026). Therefore, to ensure that corporate officers fully understand the positioning of the compensation
  in this system as a long-term incentive and link it to increased motivation, transitional measures for the portion
  granted during the term of office will be implemented at the end of FY2023 and FY2024.
     For FY2023, the medium- to long-term targets were evaluated at the end of FY2023 for the first year of the
  evaluation period from FY2021 to FY2023, and the number of shares to be granted for each corporate officer's stock-
  based compensation (portion granted during the term of office) was determined based on 1/3 of the base number of
  shares for calculation. In FY2023, the first year of transitional measures, corporate officers who have been tenured
  continuously for 3 years from June 2021 to June 2024 are eligible.

  The vested stock-based compensation (portion to be granted upon retirement) for corporate officers in FY2023 was
determined on the assumption that each corporate officer will complete his/her term of office.

      the field of dementia. In Japan, the diagnosis and treatment of patients progressed rapidly, while in the United
     States, it took some time, resulting in unachieved targets.
   ** Of the evaluation indicators for medium- to long-term targets, Company-wide materiality was evaluated only
      when each highly challenging item was fully achieved, resulting in the achievement of 3 items.

(5) Status of Share Ownership
  (i) Criteria and Concepts for Classification of Investment Stocks
       The Company's policy is to hold only shares of long-term partners that will increase mutual corporate collaboration and
     enhance corporate value, and all of its holdings are classified as investment shares for purposes other than pure investment.


  (ii) Investment shares held for purposes other than pure investment
     (A) Methods for verifying holding policies and rationality of holdings, and details of verification by the Board of Directors,
         etc. regarding the appropriateness of holding individual stocks
         The benefits and risks associated with holding these shares are verified to ensure that they are commensurate with the
       cost of capital (conservatively assumed to be 8%), and the benefits and economic rationale for increasing corporate value
       are examined by scrutinizing the net present value (NPV) based on synergy effects and other calculations, with the most
       recent verification conducted in April 2024. In addition, the details of the verification for each individual stock are
       presented at the Board of Directors meetings. It should be noted that the Company confirms that its stock holdings will be
       kept to the minimum necessary and that, in principle, the balance of its holdings will be scaled down. Specific numerical
       values of quantitative effects of holding individual stocks will not be disclosed due to the judgment that such information
       constitutes confidential business information.


     (B) Number of stocks and amounts on the balance sheet
                                        Number of Total amount shown on balance
                                          stocks      sheet (Millions of yen)

V. Financial Information
1. Basis of presentation of consolidated financial statements
   (1) The consolidated financial statements of Eisai Co., Ltd. (the “Company”) and its consolidated subsidiaries are prepared in
        accordance with International Financial Reporting Standards (hereinafter referred to as “IFRS”) pursuant to Article 93 of the
        Regulation on Terminology, Forms, and Preparation Methods of Consolidated Financial Statements (Ministry of Finance
        Order No. 28 of 1976, hereinafter referred to as the “Consolidated Financial Statement Regulations”).

2. Audit certification
  Pursuant to the provisions of Article 193-2, Paragraph 1, of the Financial Instruments and Exchange Act, the Company’s
 consolidated and non-consolidated financial statements in the Japanese version for the fiscal year (April 1, 2023 to March 31, 2024)
 were audited by Deloitte Touche Tohmatsu LLC. This document (English version) is not reviewed by Deloitte Touche Tohmatsu
 LLC. The independent auditor’s reports are not translated or included in this document.

3. Special efforts to ensure the appropriateness of consolidated financial statements, etc., and establishment of
  systems to ensure the appropriate preparation of consolidated financial statements, etc., in accordance with
  IFRS.
  The Company makes special efforts to ensure the appropriateness of its consolidated financial statements, etc., and has established
 systems to ensure the appropriate preparation of consolidated financial statements, etc., in accordance with IFRS. The details of these
 are as follows:
   (1) To accurately ascertain the content of the latest accounting standards and establish a system for appropriately addressing
       changes in these accounting standards, the Company maintains membership in the Financial Accounting Standards
        Foundation.


   (2) Regarding the application of IFRS, the Company understands the latest standards by obtaining press releases and standards
       documents issued by the International Accounting Standards Board on an ad-hoc basis. In addition, to prepare appropriate
        consolidated financial statements, etc., the Company has prepared Group accounting policies in accordance with IFRS and
        performs accounting procedures based on these policies and guidelines.

Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements
 1. Reporting Entity
    Eisai Co., Ltd. (the “Company”) is a public Japanese company listed on the Tokyo Stock Exchange Prime Market (TSE:4523).
    The Group is made up of the Company, 48 consolidated subsidiaries, and 1 equity-method affiliate and comprised of
  pharmaceutical business and other business. The Company and its subsidiaries engage in research, development, manufacturing,
  and sales of prescription drugs, over-the-counter drugs, etc. in the pharmaceutical business.


 2. Basis of Preparing Consolidated Financial Statements
   (1) Compliance
       As the Company meets the requirements of a “Specified Company” pursuant to Article 1-2 of the Consolidated Financial
    Statement Ordinance, the consolidated financial statements of the Group have been prepared in accordance with IFRS subject to
    the provisions of Article 93 of said Ordinance.


   (2) Basis of measurement
      The consolidated financial statements are prepared on an acquisition cost basis except for the financial instruments that are
    measured at fair value, assets (liabilities) of post-employment benefit plans and other factors.


   (3) Presentation currency and unit
       The consolidated financial statements are presented in Japanese yen, which is the Company’s functional currency, and figures
    less than 1 million yen are rounded to the nearest million yen.


   (4) Changes in accounting policies
       Below are the accounting policies and interpretations the Group applied for the fiscal year ended March 31, 2024. None of the
    following accounting standards and interpretations applied by the Group has any major impact on the consolidated financial
    statements for the fiscal year ended March 31, 2024.
